<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Write $55\%$ as a fraction in its lowest terms.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 55\% \\\\
&= \dfrac {55} {100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5 \times 11} {5 \times 20} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel{5} \times 11} {\cancel{5} \times 20} \\\\
&= \dfrac {11} {20}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {11} {20}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 55\% \\\\
&= \dfrac {55} {100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5 \times 11} {5 \times 20} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel{5} \times 11} {\cancel{5} \times 20} \\\\
&= \dfrac {11} {20}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</code></pre>

<div class='question subquestion'>

Write $\dfrac{3}{5}$ as a decimal.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {3} {5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 2} {5 \times 2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {6} {10} \\\\
&= 0.6
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$0.6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {3} {5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 2} {5 \times 2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {6} {10} \\\\
&= 0.6
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

\end{aligned}

<div class='question question'>

Which  of these is the largest number? (You must show your working)

- $2 + 0 \times 1 + 9$

- $2 \times 0 + 1 + 9$

- $2 + 0 \times 1 \times 9$

- $2 \times 0 + 1 + 9$

- $2 \times 0 \times 1 \times 9$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: Using BIDMAS
,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{a}    &= 2 + 0 \times 1 + 9 \\\\
            &= 2 + 0 + 9 \\\\
            &= 11 \\\\
            &= \green{11} \\\\
\text{b}    &=2 \times 0 + 1 + 9\\\\
            &= 0 + 1 + 9 \\\\
            &= \red{10} \\\\
\text{c}    &= 2 + 0 \times 1 \times 9\\\\
            &= 2 + 0 \times 9 \\\\
            &= 2 + 0 \\\\
            &= \red{2} \\\\
\text{d}    &= 2 \times 0 + 1 + 9 \\\\
            &= 0 + 1 + 9 \\\\
            &= \red{10} \\\\
\text{e}    &= 2 \times 0 \times 1 \times 9 \\\\
            &= 0 \times 1 \times 9 \\\\
            &= 0  \times 9 \\\\
            &= \red{0}
\end{aligned}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>a</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

 $2 + 0 \times 1 + 9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_bidmas,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{a}    &= 2 + 0 \times 1 + 9 \\\\
            &= 2 + 0 + 9 \\\\
            &= 11 \\\\
            &= \rtq_katex_color_answer{11} \\\\
\text{b}    &=2 \times 0 + 1 + 9\\\\
            &= 0 + 1 + 9 \\\\
            &= \rtq_katex_color_wrong_answer{10} \\\\
\text{c}    &= 2 + 0 \times 1 \times 9\\\\
            &= 2 + 0 \times 9 \\\\
            &= 2 + 0 \\\\
            &= \rtq_katex_color_wrong_answer{2} \\\\
\text{d}    &= 2 \times 0 + 1 + 9 \\\\
            &= 0 + 1 + 9 \\\\
            &= \rtq_katex_color_wrong_answer{10} \\\\
\text{e}    &= 2 \times 0 \times 1 \times 9 \\\\
            &= 0 \times 1 \times 9 \\\\
            &= 0  \times 9 \\\\
            &= \rtq_katex_color_wrong_answer{0}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>a</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

\end{aligned}
$

What is $\dfrac{1}{4}$ of ${68}?$

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {1} {4} \ \text{of} \ 68 \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {4} \times 68 \\\\
&= \dfrac {68} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {17 \times 4} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {17 \times \cancel{4}} {\cancel{4}} \\\\
&= 17
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

$ = 1\times 5\times 5$

the prime factorization of 20

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>sevenoaks-school--11-plus--maths--2010--sample-paper-1:1:5</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g2</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tnumber
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Write the correct operations ($+$ or $-$ or $\times$ or $\div$) in these statements.

- $a \ \ldots \ a = 0$

- $a \ \ldots \ a = 1$

- $a \ \ldots \ a = 2a$

- $a \ \ldots \ a = a^2$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
a \quad \boxed {\green{ - }}         \quad a     &= 0 \\\\
a \quad \boxed {\green{ \div }}      \quad a     &= 1 \\\\
a \quad \boxed {\green{ + }}         \quad a     &= 2a \\\\
a \quad \boxed {\green{ \times }}    \quad a     &= a^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$-,\div, +, \times$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
a \quad \boxed {\rtq_katex_color_answer{ - }}         \quad a     &= 0 \\\\
a \quad \boxed {\rtq_katex_color_answer{ \div }}      \quad a     &= 1 \\\\
a \quad \boxed {\rtq_katex_color_answer{ + }}         \quad a     &= 2a \\\\
a \quad \boxed {\rtq_katex_color_answer{ \times }}    \quad a     &= a^2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$-,\div, +, \times$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let's first multiply the numbers ignoring the zeroes.

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
    &       1       &   2     &   5 \\
    &   {}\times    &   1     &   6 \\
\hline
    &       7       &   5     &   0 \\

\end{array}

$= 20000$

<div class='answer'>

<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let's first multiply the numbers ignoring the zeroes.

    &       1       &   2     &   5 \\
    &   {}\times    &   1     &   6 \\
\hline
    &       7       &   5     &   0 \\
1   &       2       &   5     &   0 \\                                 
\hline
2   &       0       &   0     &   0
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>

<div class='question question'>

Write down a number that is smaller than $0.8$ and bigger than $\dfrac {5} {7}$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times (0.8 + \dfrac {5} {7}) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times (0.8 + 0.71) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times (1.51) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1.51} {2} \\\\
&= 0.75 \\\\ 
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$0.75$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times (1.51) \\\\

\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

&= \dfrac {523 \times \cancel{7} \times \cancel{2}} {\cancel{7} \times \cancel{2}} \\\\
&= 523
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$523$

</div>

<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

\begin{aligned}
&= 7322 \div 14 \\\\
&= \dfrac {7322} {14} \\\\
&= \dfrac {523 \times 7 \times 2} {7 \times 2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {523 \times \cancel{7} \times \cancel{2}} {\cancel{7} \times \cancel{2}} \\\\
&= 523
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Using exactly four fours, any of the operations $+ - \times \div$  make an expression whose value is $5$.

Example: 

$0 = 4 \div 4 \times 4 - 4$  

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$((4 \times 4) + 4) \div 4 = 5$

Let's verify.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= ((4 \times 4) + 4) \div 4 \\\\
&= (16 + 4) \div 4 \\\\
&= 20 \div 4 \\\\
&= 5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$((4 \times 4) + 4) \div 4 = 5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$((4 \times 4) + 4) \div 4 = 5$
<emptyline>
Let's verify.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= ((4 \times 4) + 4) \div 4 \\\\
&= (16 + 4) \div 4 \\\\
&= 20 \div 4 \\\\
&= 5
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

Let's simplify the left hand side value.

$
\begin {aligned}
&= \dfrac {6} {10} \\\\
&= 0.6
\end {aligned}

Let's simplify the right hand side value.

$
\begin {aligned}
&= \dfrac {3} {4} \\\\
&= 0.75
\end {aligned}
$

</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

$\dfrac {1} {3}$ of $36$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {1} {3} \ \text {of} \ 36 \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {3} \times 36 \\\\
&= \dfrac {36} {3} \\\\
&= \dfrac{12 \times 3} {3} \\\\
&= \dfrac{12 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel{3}} \\\\
&= 12
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

&= \dfrac {36} {3} \\\\
&= \dfrac{12 \times 3} {3} \\\\
&= \dfrac{12 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel{3}} \\\\
&= 12
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {4} {5} \ \text {of} \ 80 \\\\
&= \dfrac {4} {5} \times 80 \\\\
&= \dfrac {4 \times 80} {5} \\\\
&= \dfrac{4 \times 5 \times 16} {5} \\\\
&= \dfrac{4 \times \cancel{5} \times 16} {\cancel{5}} \\\\
&= 4 \times 16 \\\\
&= 64
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

$8 + 5 \times 21$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: Using BIDMAS
,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 8 + 5 \times 21 \\\\
&= 8 + 105 \\\\
&= 113
\end{aligned}
$

The rest of the members are children.

</div>
<div class='workings'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What percentage of the members are women?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{\% of women}  &= \dfrac {2} {5} \times 100  \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {2 \times 100} {5}  \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {2 \times 20 \times 5} {5}  \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {2 \times 20\times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}}  \\\\
                    &= 2\times20 \\\\
                    &= 40 \%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$40 \%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{\% of women}  &= \dfrac {2} {5} \times 100  \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {2 \times 100} {5}  \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {2 \times 20 \times 5} {5}  \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {2 \times 20\times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}}  \\\\
                    &= 2\times20 \\\\
                    &= 40 \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$40 \%$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many of the members are men?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of men}    &= \dfrac {1} {3} \text { of } 480 \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times 480 \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {480} {3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 160} {3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel{3}\times160} {\cancel{3}} \\\\
                        &= 160 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$160$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of men}    &= \dfrac {1} {3} \text { of } 480 \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times 480 \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {480} {3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 160} {3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel{3}\times160} {\cancel{3}} \\\\
                        &= 160 
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many of the members are children?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of children}   &= \text {Total} - \text {Women}- \text{Men} \\\\
                            &= 480 - \dfrac {2} {5} \times 480 - 160 \\\\
                            &= 480 - \dfrac {2 \times 480} {5}  -160 \\\\
                            &= 480 - \dfrac {2 \times 96 \times 5} {5}  -160 \\\\
                            &= 480 - \dfrac {2 \times 96 \times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}}  -160 \\\\
                            &= 480 - 192 - 160 \\\\
                            &= 128
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$128$

</div>

                            &= 480 - \dfrac {2} {5} \times 480 - 160 \\\\
                            &= 480 - \dfrac {2 \times 480} {5}  -160 \\\\
                            &= 480 - \dfrac {2 \times 96 \times 5} {5}  -160 \\\\
                            &= 480 - \dfrac {2 \times 96 \times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}}  -160 \\\\
                            &= 480 - 192 - 160 \\\\
                            &= 128
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

<div class='question question'>

Here is a sum with some signs missing

$2  \quad 3  \quad 4 = 10$

You can make it correct by putting in the correct signs:

$2 \times 3 + 4 = 10$

Fill in the gaps below to make the sums correct.

You may use any of the signs $+, -, \times, \div$ and you can use brackets if you wish. 

</div>

Let's verify.

$
\begin{aligned}

&= 32 + 2 \\\\
&= 34 
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question question'>

A rule for numbers is to add $12$ and divide the answer by $2$.

For example if you start with $10$ you add $12$ and get $22$ and then you divide by $2$ to get $11$.

So applying the rule to the number $10$ you get the answer $11$. 

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the answer if you apply the rule to the number $30 \text{?}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= (30 + 12) \div 2 \\\\
&= \dfrac {30 + 12} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {42} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {21 \times 2} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {21 \times \cancel{2}} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
&= 21
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$21$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= (30 + 12) \div 2 \\\\
&= \dfrac {30 + 12} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {42} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {21 \times 2} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {21 \times \cancel{2}} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
&= 21
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$21$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the number if you apply the rule twice starting with the number $20 \text{?}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{1}{2} \times \bigg (\dfrac{20+12}{2} + 12 \bigg ) \\\\
&= \dfrac{1}{2} \times \bigg (\dfrac{32}{2} + 12 \bigg ) \\\\
&= \dfrac{1}{2} \times (16+12) \\\\
&= \dfrac{28}{2} \\\\
&=  14
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$14$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{1}{2} \times \bigg (\dfrac{20+12}{2} + 12 \bigg ) \\\\
&= \dfrac{1}{2} \times \bigg (\dfrac{32}{2} + 12 \bigg ) \\\\
&= \dfrac{1}{2} \times (16+12) \\\\
&= \dfrac{28}{2} \\\\
&=  14
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$14$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If the rule is applied to a number the answer is $15$. What was the starting number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the starting number $ = a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac{a+12}{2} &= 15\\\\
a+12            &= 15\times2\\\\
a+12            &= 30\\\\
a               &= 30-12\\\\
a               &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$18$

</div>

a               &= 18
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If the rule is applied twice the answer is $50$. What was the starting number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the starting number $ = a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac{1}{2} \times \bigg (\dfrac{a+12}{2} + 12\bigg )  &= 50\\\\
\dfrac{a+12}{2} + 12                                    &= 50 \times 2 \\\\
\dfrac{a+12}{2} + 12                                    &= 100 \\\\
\dfrac{a+12}{2}                                         &= 100-12 \\\\
\dfrac{a+12}{2}                                         &= 88 \\\\
a+12                                                    &= 88 \times 2 \\\\
a+12                                                    &= 176 \\\\
a                                                       &= 176-12 \\\\ 
a                                                       &= 164
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$164$

</div>

\end{aligned}
